I don't know, I haven't really noticed it being an issue. I haven't seen them as much lately, but I think that's just because everyone is so busy. My buddy Dave, he just got this new job working at a brewery across town. He's learning how to operate these massive stainless steel fermentation tanks. He was telling me about the whole brewing process the other day, how they have to carefully control the temperature when they add the yeast, otherwise it completely changes the flavor profile of the beer. Yeast is actually fascinating, it's a living organism, you know? My grandmother used to bake her own bread every Sunday, and she had this sourdough starter that she kept alive for, like, twenty years. She kept it in this old ceramic crock on the kitchen counter. It smelled so sour, kind of like vinegar but sweeter. She made the best sandwiches with that bread, usually with leftover roast beef from dinner the night before. She always used this really sharp horseradish mustard that would clear your sinuses right out...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't think my focus stuff is messing up my friendships. We just haven't hung out much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focusing and staying on task is something that you've experienced throughout your life, or is it a relatively new development?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely new. I mean, I've always been a little scatterbrained, but not like this. This whole wired, completely out-of-control stretch... it just started right after I won this award at a big jewelry show. Which was amazing, by the way. It was a regional show, they held it in this huge convention center downtown. The lighting in those places is always terrible for displaying jewelry, it's all fluorescent overheads that wash out the stones. You really need halogen or good LED spot lighting to make the facets pop, especially on diamonds or sapphires. I had this one piece, a custom sapphire pendant, and I actually brought my own little battery-powered display lights just to make sure it looked right. Those little lights take these tiny button batteries, the kind that cost like five dollars each at the pharmacy. I always wonder who invented those. They're so specific. My watch uses one of those too, it's an old mechanical watch my grandfather gave me. Well, it's a quartz movement, so not truly mechanical, but it has that vintage look. He bought it in Switzerland back in the seventies when he was traveling for work. He used to sell industrial farm equipment, like those massive combine harvesters. I saw one of those up close once at a state fair, the tires on it were taller than I am. Yeah, the details are just... they're slipping right past me. It's so frustrating because my whole job is details. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. Sapphires are actually really tricky to match, you know? People think they're just blue, but there are so many different shades. You've got your cornflower blue, your royal blue, and then the ones from Montana that have this almost steely, greenish tint to them. I actually really like the Montana ones. My uncle lives out in Montana, not too far from Yellowstone. He's got a cabin out there. We went to visit him when I was in high school and we saw this huge herd of bison just walking right down the middle of the road, stopping all the traffic. They're massive animals, it's terrifying being that close to them in a little rental car. The rental car was this tiny silver sedan, the air conditioning barely worked. Which was miserable because it was July and it gets surprisingly hot out there during the day. You don't think of Montana being hot, but the sun just beats down on you... 

But yeah, I look at the stones and the tickets and I just can't keep the numbers straight in my head to actually do the work.

Mostly FedEx, but yesterday it was this massive flatbed truck, and I always wonder how they navigate those tight corners downtown, you know? The city really needs to fix the parking situation on 4th Avenue, it's a nightmare if you drive a sedan, let alone a rig like that. \n\nBut, uh... wait, yeah. So I'll jump from the saw mark to the email to the noise outside, and then I look down and realize I’ve completely lost the whole work order I was just holding. It's just... I can't keep my focus straight.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're not working on a task?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Um, I don't really think so. Like, if I'm not working, I'm usually just trying to chill, you know? Like sitting on the couch. I actually just got this new couch, it's one of those modular ones where you can move all the pieces around to make a huge pit. My buddy Mike helped me bring it up the stairs, which was a total nightmare because the stairwell in my building has this weird sharp turn on the second floor. The landlord said he was going to fix the lighting in there too, because it's super dark, and someone is definitely going to trip and sue him. I almost dropped a whole gallon of milk there last week, which would have been a disaster to clean up on that old carpet. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, restless. Yeah, no, I don't really notice myself getting restless or fidgety when I'm just sitting around.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Impulsive? Um... not really, no. I mean, I guess I just do what I normally do. Like, I don't just go out and do crazy things without thinking. Actually, speaking of doing things, I just got this new polishing compound for the shop. It's supposed to be this revolutionary formula from Germany, and I had to special order it because the local supply place stopped carrying the good stuff. They said it was a supply chain issue, which is what everyone says now, right? It's like you can't even get decent sandpaper without waiting three weeks. My dad used to say that back in the day, you could walk into any hardware store and get exactly what you needed, made right here in the States. He had this old Chevy truck he'd drive to the hardware store every Saturday... \n\nBut, uh, yeah. No, I don't really do things on impulse. I just stick to my routine, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talking more than others? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I talk a normal amount, I guess. Like when I'm at the shop, I talk to customers about their pieces. I had this one lady come in yesterday, actually, wanting her grandmother's wedding band resized. It was this beautiful Art Deco piece, platinum with these tiny little milgrain details on the edges. You don't really see craftsmanship like that anymore, everything is so mass-produced now, you know? It's like people just go to the mall and buy whatever is in the case. The lighting in those mall stores is so intense, too, it makes even the cheap diamonds look like they have good clarity. I always tell people you have to look at a stone in natural daylight to really see what you're getting. \n\nBut, um... wait, what was the question? Oh, talking a lot. Yeah, no, I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything. I just talk like anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finishing tasks you start?\n\nPatient: Trouble finishing things? Yeah, actually, that's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Like, my mind just won't stay on one thing anymore. I'll be at my bench, right? Working on a piece. And I'll see a customer email pop up on my phone, so I look at that, and then I notice a saw mark on the silver I'm holding that I need to file down, but then there's this loud noise outside... \n\nSpeaking of noises outside, they've been doing construction on the street in front of the shop for like three weeks now. They're tearing up the whole water main, I think. The guy operating the backhoe is actually pretty incredible to watch, he's got this crazy precision with it. He scooped up this giant chunk of asphalt yesterday without even scraping the curb. My uncle used to operate heavy machinery like that, he worked on the Big Dig up in Boston for years. He always said the union guys had it made, but the hours were brutal. He'd be up at like 3 AM every day, drinking that awful instant coffee...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'll hear a noise outside, and the next thing I know, I've completely lost the whole work order I was supposed to be doing. I just can't seem to get to the end of anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble meeting deadlines or fulfilling your responsibilities at work due to your difficulty with focusing?\n\nPatient: Deadlines? Yeah... yeah, that's been really bad actually. I just missed a major custom delivery, which is terrible for business. And my shop records got so completely disorganized that I actually had to stop taking new work for now. I usually write all my orders down in this old leather-bound ledger my grandfather gave me. He wasn't a jeweler, he was a cobbler, worked with leather his whole life. His hands were basically like thick hide by the time he retired. He used to make these custom riding boots for guys on motorcycles—real heavy-duty stuff with steel shanks built right into the soles. I don't know how those guys ride without getting tinnitus, honestly. My buddy Dave bought a Harley last summer and he spent like three grand just changing out the exhaust pipes so it would sound louder... \n\nBut, um... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, work. Yeah, because things got so disorganized and I stopped taking new orders, my family has actually been checking on me a lot more lately. I guess they got really worried about how far I'd slipped from my usual routines. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of my responsibilities.\n\nDoctor: Are you having problems with your relationships with family or friends because of your difficulty with focusing?\n\nPatient: Family and friends? Well, like I just mentioned, my family is definitely hovering a lot more right now. They saw how my routines were completely falling apart, you know, with the shop being so disorganized and me missing that big custom delivery... \n\nSpeaking of deliveries, I had to wait like four hours for the FedEx guy yesterday. Usually, it's this guy named Carl, he's great, always makes sure to scan things right in front of me so I have the tracking updated immediately. He's got this rescue dog that rides in the truck with him sometimes, a little terrier mix. I think terriers are just the smartest dogs. My aunt had a Jack Russell when I was growing up, and that dog could literally open the latch on the screen door by itself. It would just jump up and hit the handle with its paw. We had to start locking the main door because it kept letting itself out to chase the squirrels in the yard. The squirrels around here are getting so bold, too, they'll come right up to the porch...\n\nWait, sorry, relationships. Yeah, so because I had to stop taking new work and everything got so chaotic, my family is just really worried. They check in on me constantly now. It's frustrating, but I guess I understand why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go or have a lot of excess energy?\n\nPatient: Excess energy? Yeah, man, it's like I have a motor running inside me right now. I literally couldn't sit still at my bench. I'd bounce from sketching out this new watch case idea to emailing my suppliers... \n\nSpeaking of suppliers, my main silver guy is down in the Diamond District in New York. Have you ever been down there? It is absolutely wild. Just shoulder-to-shoulder people haggling over stones and metal on the street. I went down there a couple years ago and got this massive pastrami sandwich at this tiny deli right off 47th street. The guy behind the counter was screaming at everybody, but like, in a fun way, you know? Classic New York. My cousin actually lived in Brooklyn for a bit, but he moved back home because the rent was just insane. He was paying like two grand for a place the size of a walk-in closet. He had this orange tabby cat that would constantly knock his pots and pans off the kitchen shelf because there was literally nowhere else for it to climb in the apartment. Cats are so weird like that, they just have to be up high...\n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, being on the go. Yeah, so I'd be sketching and emailing the suppliers, and then I'd end up just completely rearranging all the display trays in the front of the shop long after closing time. I just couldn't stop moving or turn my brain off to go home.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by your inability to focus and its effects on your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: Overwhelmed? Yeah... yeah, definitely. I mean, like I was saying earlier, it's just a lot all at once. Missing that major custom delivery was a huge blow, and having to completely stop taking new work because my shop records are a disaster... it's incredibly stressful. I actually misplaced a whole parcel of Montana sapphires the other day because my bench is such a mess right now. \n\nHave you ever seen a Montana sapphire? They aren't just blue, they have this crazy teal, almost greenish tint to them. They mine them up in the mountains out there. My uncle actually went out to Montana a few years ago—not for gems, but for fly fishing. He spent like two weeks just standing in rivers wearing those huge rubber waders. I don't know how they don't freeze to death doing that, the water comes straight off the glaciers. He caught this massive trout and had it taxidermied. It's hanging in his basement right next to his pool table. We used to play pool down there all the time when I was in high school. I was actually pretty good, I could run the table on him sometimes if I got a good break... \n\nBut, um... sorry. Overwhelmed. Right. Yeah, so between the shop falling apart and my family constantly checking in on me because they're so worried about my routines slipping... yeah, it's definitely overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Interrupt people? I don't really think I do that. At least, nobody's told me I do. I usually let people finish what they're saying... \n\nActually, my buddy Dave, he's the one who interrupts all the time. You can't get a word in edgewise with him. We were at this bar the other night, this place downtown that has these old arcade cabinets in the back. They had a vintage Pac-Man machine, the original one with the joystick that always sticks a little to the left. I used to play that for hours at the laundromat when my mom was washing our clothes. The laundromat always smelled like bleach and those cheap fabric softener sheets, you know? There was this one lady who worked there, she always had a pocket full of quarters and would give me a couple if I helped her fold towels. Towels are the easiest thing to fold, honestly, way better than fitted sheets. I don't think anyone actually knows how to fold a fitted sheet properly, you just kind of roll it into a ball and shove it in the linen closet and hope for the best...\n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that. I think I'm pretty good at letting people talk.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus in quiet environments, like when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Quiet environments? Yeah, actually, it's really bad when I'm just alone at the shop or at home. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. It's like my brain just slips off the numbers. \n\nSpeaking of numbers, I used to be so good at math in high school. I had this teacher, Mr. Henderson, he was this huge guy who used to play college football, I think for Ohio State or something. He had this massive class ring that he would always tap on the whiteboard when he was explaining equations. It made this loud *clack clack* sound. He actually drove this vintage Mustang, cherry red, completely restored it himself. I've always wanted to restore a classic car, maybe an old Chevy C10 pickup. The ones from the late 60s with the rounded hoods, those are just beautiful. You could throw a couple dirt bikes in the back of one of those and just drive out to the desert for the weekend. I went dirt biking once with my cousin out in Nevada, and I completely wiped out into this patch of scrub brush. I was pulling thorns out of my jeans for like three days... \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, right, focusing when it's quiet. Yeah, it's just really hard to keep a thought straight right now, even when there's no distractions. Um... I guess maybe? I haven't really noticed making any huge mistakes, at least not more than usual. I mean, my bench is a mess, but the actual work... I think I'm still doing okay with it. \n\nI did drop a tiny piece of silver wire the other day, though. It rolled right off the edge of the desk and vanished. Silver is getting so expensive lately, too. The market prices just keep fluctuating. I was reading this article about how silver mining in South America is being affected by these massive rainstorms they've been having. It's crazy how weather patterns can just completely disrupt an entire industry like that. Speaking of rain, it poured here last Tuesday, like, absolute buckets. The gutters on my house were totally overflowing. I really need to get up on a ladder and clean those out. The last time I was on a ladder, I was helping my neighbor string up some patio lights. He bought these really nice Edison bulbs, the kind with the visible filaments. They look great at night, really gives his backyard this cool, vintage vibe. He likes to grill out there, makes these amazing ribs with a dry rub...\n\nWait, sorry. Mistakes in my work. Yeah, I don't really think I'm making careless mistakes, honestly. Just... mostly struggling to keep things organized.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like your inability to focus is affecting your overall well-being or daily life in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad. I missed a major custom delivery recently, which I never do. And my shop records got so completely disorganized that I actually had to stop taking new work for now just to try and figure out what's going on. \n\nIt's weird how fast paper can pile up, you know? Like, I used to keep everything in these manila folders, color-coded by the type of metal. I bought this huge pack of folders from an office supply store that was going out of business. It was one of those big box places, they had signs up everywhere, like \"Everything Must Go.\" I ended up buying a ton of stuff I didn't need, like three staplers and a whole box of those little binder clips. Binder clips are actually super useful for holding pieces together when you're soldering, if you don't have the right clamps. Soldering is probably my favorite part of the job, honestly. You have to get the torch just right, get the metal to that perfect cherry-red color before the solder flows. If you heat it too much, the whole piece just melts into a puddle. I melted a silver ring once when I was first starting out, just completely ruined it. The guy I apprenticed under, he just laughed and told me to throw it in the scrap pile. He had this massive scrap pile in the corner of his shop, must have been worth thousands of dollars just sitting there...\n\nOh, right, my daily life. Yeah, so I had to stop taking new work. And my family has been checking on me a lot more lately, too. I guess they got worried about how far I’d slipped from my usual routines. I'm usually pretty on top of things, but right now it's just... it's just really hard to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're doing two or more things at the same time, like working on a task and also thinking about something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. That's actually... that's kind of what really brought me in. My mind just will not stay on one thing anymore. I'll be sitting at the bench, right? And I'll be looking at a customer email on my phone, but then I notice a tiny little saw mark on a bezel I was working on, so I pick that up. And then I hear a truck backing up outside, and I start wondering if it's the delivery guy with the new polishing wheels I ordered. \n\nI ordered those wheels from this supplier in New York. They have this massive catalog, like, it's literally the size of a phone book. Do they even make phone books anymore? I remember when I was a kid, my mom used to use the old yellow pages as a booster seat for me at the kitchen table. We had this big oak table, super heavy. My dad bought it at an estate sale from this old lady who used to breed show dogs. Poodles, I think. Standard poodles, not the little toy ones. The standard ones are actually really smart, they used to be hunting dogs in Germany or somewhere. I watched a documentary about that once. They would shave the back half of the dog so it wouldn't get waterlogged when it was swimming after ducks, but they left the fur around the joints to keep them warm. It's crazy how those haircuts started out as something practical and now it's just for show...\n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the bench. Yeah, so I jump from the email to the saw mark to the noise outside, and by the time I look back down, I realize I’ve completely lost the whole work order. I just can't keep my focus on what I'm actually supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain your relationships with friends due to your current struggles with focus and organization?\n\nPatient: Relationships with friends? I don't know, I haven't really noticed it being an issue. I haven't seen them as much lately, but I think that's just because everyone is so busy. My buddy Dave, he just got this new job working at a brewery across town. He's learning how to operate these massive stainless steel fermentation tanks. He was telling me about the whole brewing process the other day, how they have to carefully control the temperature when they add the yeast, otherwise it completely changes the flavor profile of the beer. Yeast is actually fascinating, it's a living organism, you know? My grandmother used to bake her own bread every Sunday, and she had this sourdough starter that she kept alive for, like, twenty years. She kept it in this old ceramic crock on the kitchen counter. It smelled so sour, kind of like vinegar but sweeter. She made the best sandwiches with that bread, usually with leftover roast beef from dinner the night before. She always used this really sharp horseradish mustard that would clear your sinuses right out...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't think my focus stuff is messing up my friendships. We just haven't hung out much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focusing and staying on task is something that you've experienced throughout your life, or is it a relatively new development?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely new. I mean, I've always been a little scatterbrained, but not like this. This whole wired, completely out-of-control stretch... it just started right after I won this award at a big jewelry show. Which was amazing, by the way. It was a regional show, they held it in this huge convention center downtown. The lighting in those places is always terrible for displaying jewelry, it's all fluorescent overheads that wash out the stones. You really need halogen or good LED spot lighting to make the facets pop, especially on diamonds or sapphires. I had this one piece, a custom sapphire pendant, and I actually brought my own little battery-powered display lights just to make sure it looked right. Those little lights take these tiny button batteries, the kind that cost like five dollars each at the pharmacy. I always wonder who invented those. They're so specific. My watch uses one of those too, it's an old mechanical watch my grandfather gave me. Well, it's a quartz movement, so not truly mechanical, but it has that vintage look. He bought it in Switzerland back in the seventies when he was traveling for work. He used to sell industrial farm equipment, like those massive combine harvesters. I saw one of those up close once at a state fair, the tires on it were taller than I am. They had this whole display of antique tractors too, painted bright green and red...\n\nOh, but yeah, the focus thing. It started right after that jewelry show award, and it went on for about two weeks straight where I was just completely wired like that. So no, it's not a lifelong thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to pay attention to details or make careless mistakes in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the details are just... they're slipping right past me. It's so frustrating because my whole job is details. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. Sapphires are actually really tricky to match, you know? People think they're just blue, but there are so many different shades. You've got your cornflower blue, your royal blue, and then the ones from Montana that have this almost steely, greenish tint to them. I actually really like the Montana ones. My uncle lives out in Montana, not too far from Yellowstone. He's got a cabin out there. We went to visit him when I was in high school and we saw this huge herd of bison just walking right down the middle of the road, stopping all the traffic. They're massive animals, it's terrifying being that close to them in a little rental car. The rental car was this tiny silver sedan, the air conditioning barely worked. Which was miserable because it was July and it gets surprisingly hot out there during the day. You don't think of Montana being hot, but the sun just beats down on you... \n\nBut yeah, I look at the stones and the tickets and I just can't keep the numbers straight in my head to actually do the work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. My cousin actually lived in Brooklyn for a bit, but he moved back home because the rent was just insane. He was paying like two grand for a place the size of a walk-in closet. He had this orange tabby cat that would constantly knock his pots and pans off the kitchen shelf because there was literally nowhere else for it to climb in the apartment. Cats are so weird like that, they just have to be up high...\n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right, being on the go. Yeah, so I'd be sketching and emailing the suppliers, and then I'd end up just completely rearranging all the display trays in the front of the shop long after closing time. I just couldn't stop moving or turn my brain off to go home.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed by your inability to focus and its effects on your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: Overwhelmed? Yeah... yeah, definitely. I mean, like I was saying earlier, it's just a lot all at once. Missing that major custom delivery was a huge blow, and having to completely stop taking new work because my shop records are a disaster... it's incredibly stressful. I actually misplaced a whole parcel of Montana sapphires the other day because my bench is such a mess right now. \n\nHave you ever seen a Montana sapphire? They aren't just blue, they have this crazy teal, almost greenish tint to them. They mine them up in the mountains out there. My uncle actually went out to Montana a few years ago—not for gems, but for fly fishing. He spent like two weeks just standing in rivers wearing those huge rubber waders. I don't know how they don't freeze to death doing that, the water comes straight off the glaciers. He caught this massive trout and had it taxidermied. It's hanging in his basement right next to his pool table. We used to play pool down there all the time when I was in high school. I was actually pretty good, I could run the table on him sometimes if I got a good break... \n\nBut, um... sorry. Overwhelmed. Right. Yeah, so between the shop falling apart and my family constantly checking in on me because they're so worried about my routines slipping... yeah, it's definitely overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Interrupt people? I don't really think I do that. At least, nobody's told me I do. I usually let people finish what they're saying... \n\nActually, my buddy Dave, he's the one who interrupts all the time. You can't get a word in edgewise with him. We were at this bar the other night, this place downtown that has these old arcade cabinets in the back. They had a vintage Pac-Man machine, the original one with the joystick that always sticks a little to the left. I used to play that for hours at the laundromat when my mom was washing our clothes. The laundromat always smelled like bleach and those cheap fabric softener sheets, you know? There was this one lady who worked there, she always had a pocket full of quarters and would give me a couple if I helped her fold towels. Towels are the easiest thing to fold, honestly, way better than fitted sheets. I don't think anyone actually knows how to fold a fitted sheet properly, you just kind of roll it into a ball and shove it in the linen closet and hope for the best...\n\nWait, what were you asking? Oh, interrupting. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that. Yeah, actually, it's really bad when I'm just alone at the shop or at home. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. It's like my brain just slips off the numbers. \n\nSpeaking of numbers, I used to be so good at math in high school. I had this teacher, Mr. Henderson, he was this huge guy who used to play college football, I think for Ohio State or something. He had this massive class ring that he would always tap on the whiteboard when he was explaining equations. It made this loud *clack clack* sound. He actually drove this vintage Mustang, cherry red, completely restored it himself. I've always wanted to restore a classic car, maybe an old Chevy C10 pickup. The ones from the late 60s with the rounded hoods, those are just beautiful. You could throw a couple dirt bikes in the back of one of those and just drive out to the desert for the weekend. I went dirt biking once with my cousin out in Nevada, and I completely wiped out into this patch of scrub brush. Yeah, it's just really hard to keep a thought straight right now, even when there's no distractions. I think I'm still doing okay with it. \n\nI did drop a tiny piece of silver wire the other day, though. It rolled right off the edge of the desk and vanished. Silver is getting so expensive lately, too. The market prices just keep fluctuating. It's crazy how weather patterns can just completely disrupt an entire industry like that. Speaking of rain, it poured here last Tuesday, like, absolute buckets. He likes to grill out there, makes these amazing ribs with a dry rub...\n\nWait, sorry. Mistakes in my work. Yeah, I don't really think I'm making careless mistakes, honestly. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad. I missed a major custom delivery recently, which I never do. And my shop records got so completely disorganized that I actually had to stop taking new work for now just to try and figure out what's going on. \n\nIt's weird how fast paper can pile up, you know? Like, I used to keep everything in these manila folders, color-coded by the type of metal. I bought this huge pack of folders from an office supply store that was going out of business. It was one of those big box places, they had signs up everywhere, like \"Everything Must Go.\" I ended up buying a ton of stuff I didn't need, like three staplers and a whole box of those little binder clips. Binder clips are actually super useful for holding pieces together when you're soldering, if you don't have the right clamps. Soldering is probably my favorite part of the job, honestly. You have to get the torch just right, get the metal to that perfect cherry-red color before the solder flows. If you heat it too much, the whole piece just melts into a puddle. I melted a silver ring once when I was first starting out, just completely ruined it. The guy I apprenticed under, he just laughed and told me to throw it in the scrap pile. He had this massive scrap pile in the corner of his shop, must have been worth thousands of dollars just sitting there...\n\nOh, right, my daily life. Yeah, so I had to stop taking new work. I'm usually pretty on top of things, but right now it's just... it's just really hard to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're doing two or more things at the same time, like working on a task and also thinking about something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. Yeast is actually fascinating, it's a living organism, you know? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Yeah, definitely. During that whole stretch, I just... I couldn't sit still at the bench at all. I'd be bouncing around, you know? Like, I'd start sketching out this new watch case idea, and then two minutes later I'm emailing suppliers, and then I'm out on the floor rearranging the display trays, and I'd be doing that long after we closed for the day. Displays are actually really important, you have to group things by metal type or it looks cluttered. White gold and platinum need to be separated from the yellow gold. Although rose gold is getting really popular again. It's got copper mixed in, that's what gives it that pink color. My sister bought this huge set of copper pots and pans for her kitchen. They look beautiful hanging up, but they're a nightmare to keep clean. You have to polish them constantly or they tarnish and get all dull. She uses this special paste, it smells awful, kind of like rotten eggs. She made me help her polish them right before Thanksgiving dinner last year. We had this massive turkey, and my mom made her famous stuffing with the sage and sausage...

But yeah, I was just constantly moving, bouncing from one thing to the next, long after I should have gone home.

It's so frustrating because my whole job is details. Sapphires are actually really tricky to match, you know? People think they're just blue, but there are so many different shades. You've got your cornflower blue, your royal blue, and then the ones from Montana that have this almost steely, greenish tint to them. I actually really like the Montana ones. It's supposed to be this revolutionary formula from Germany, and I had to special order it because the local supply place stopped carrying the good stuff. They said it was a supply chain issue, which is what everyone says now, right? It's like you can't even get decent sandpaper without waiting three weeks. My dad used to say that back in the day, you could walk into any hardware store and get exactly what you needed, made right here in the States. He had this old Chevy truck he'd drive to the hardware store every Saturday... \n\nBut, uh, yeah. No, I don't really do things on impulse. I just stick to my routine, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Talking more than others? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I talk a normal amount, I guess. Like when I'm at the shop, I talk to customers about their pieces. I had this one lady come in yesterday, actually, wanting her grandmother's wedding band resized. It was this beautiful Art Deco piece, platinum with these tiny little milgrain details on the edges. You don't really see craftsmanship like that anymore, everything is so mass-produced now, you know? It's like people just go to the mall and buy whatever is in the case. The lighting in those mall stores is so intense, too, it makes even the cheap diamonds look like they have good clarity. I always tell people you have to look at a stone in natural daylight to really see what you're getting. \n\nBut, um... wait, what was the question? Oh, talking a lot. Yeah, no, I don't think I dominate the conversation or anything. I just talk like anybody else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finishing tasks you start?\n\nPatient: Trouble finishing things? Yeah, actually, that's... that's kind of the main reason I'm here. Like, my mind just won't stay on one thing anymore. I'll be at my bench, right? Working on a piece. And I'll see a customer email pop up on my phone, so I look at that, and then I notice a saw mark on the silver I'm holding that I need to file down, but then there's this loud noise outside... \n\nSpeaking of noises outside, they've been doing construction on the street in front of the shop for like three weeks now. They're tearing up the whole water main, I think. The guy operating the backhoe is actually pretty incredible to watch, he's got this crazy precision with it. He scooped up this giant chunk of asphalt yesterday without even scraping the curb. My uncle used to operate heavy machinery like that, he worked on the Big Dig up in Boston for years. He always said the union guys had it made, but the hours were brutal. He'd be up at like 3 AM every day, drinking that awful instant coffee...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'll hear a noise outside, and the next thing I know, I've completely lost the whole work order I was supposed to be doing. He wasn't a jeweler, he was a cobbler, worked with leather his whole life. His hands were basically like thick hide by the time he retired. He used to make these custom riding boots for guys on motorcycles—real heavy-duty stuff with steel shanks built right into the soles. He had this orange tabby cat that would constantly knock his pots and pans off the kitchen shelf because there was literally nowhere else for it to climb in the apartment. They aren't just blue, they have this crazy teal, almost greenish tint to them. They mine them up in the mountains out there. I don't really think I do that. At least, nobody's told me I do. I usually let people finish what they're saying... \n\nActually, my buddy Dave, he's the one who interrupts all the time. The laundromat always smelled like bleach and those cheap fabric softener sheets, you know? There was this one lady who worked there, she always had a pocket full of quarters and would give me a couple if I helped her fold towels. Oh, interrupting. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that. I think I'm pretty good at letting people talk.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus in quiet environments, like when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Quiet environments? Yeah, actually, it's really bad when I'm just alone at the shop or at home. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. It's definitely not all the time. It comes in phases, I guess. Like, that whole wired, totally out-of-control stretch where I couldn't focus on anything? That started right after I won this award at a big jewelry show, and it lasted for about two weeks straight. The show was down in Miami, which was wild. I'd never been to Miami before. Thrift stores are great for finding weird, unique stuff. I once found this old brass pocket watch from the 1920s in a bin for like five bucks. Yeah, so between the shop falling apart and my family constantly checking in on me because they're so worried about my routines slipping... yeah, it's definitely overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Interrupt people? I don't really think I do that. At least, nobody's told me I do. I usually let people finish what they're saying... \n\nActually, my buddy Dave, he's the one who interrupts all the time. You can't get a word in edgewise with him. We were at this bar the other night, this place downtown that has these old arcade cabinets in the back. Oh, interrupting. Yeah, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that. I think I'm pretty good at letting people talk.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus in quiet environments, like when you're alone at home?\n\nPatient: Quiet environments? Yeah, actually, it's really bad when I'm just alone at the shop or at home. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. Yeah, it's just really hard to keep a thought straight right now, even when there's no distractions. I think I'm still doing okay with it. \n\nI did drop a tiny piece of silver wire the other day, though. It rolled right off the edge of the desk and vanished. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad. I missed a major custom delivery recently, which I never do. And my shop records got so completely disorganized that I actually had to stop taking new work for now just to try and figure out what's going on. \n\nIt's weird how fast paper can pile up, you know? Like, I used to keep everything in these manila folders, color-coded by the type of metal. I bought this huge pack of folders from an office supply store that was going out of business. Binder clips are actually super useful for holding pieces together when you're soldering, if you don't have the right clamps. Soldering is probably my favorite part of the job, honestly. You have to get the torch just right, get the metal to that perfect cherry-red color before the solder flows. If you heat it too much, the whole piece just melts into a puddle. He actually drove this vintage Mustang, cherry red, completely restored it himself. I've always wanted to restore a classic car, maybe an old Chevy C10 pickup. The ones from the late 60s with the rounded hoods, those are just beautiful. You could throw a couple dirt bikes in the back of one of those and just drive out to the desert for the weekend. I went dirt biking once with my cousin out in Nevada, and I completely wiped out into this patch of scrub brush. I was pulling thorns out of my jeans for like three days... \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Oh, right, focusing when it's quiet. Yeah, it's just really hard to keep a thought straight right now, even when there's no distractions. I just look at the stones and my mind goes completely blank on what I'm supposed to be doing with them.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you make careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: Careless mistakes? Um... I guess maybe? I haven't really noticed making any huge mistakes, at least not more than usual. I mean, my bench is a mess, but the actual work... I think I'm still doing okay with it. \n\nI did drop a tiny piece of silver wire the other day, though. It rolled right off the edge of the desk and vanished. Silver is getting so expensive lately, too. The market prices just keep fluctuating. I was reading this article about how silver mining in South America is being affected by these massive rainstorms they've been having. It's crazy how weather patterns can just completely disrupt an entire industry like that. Speaking of rain, it poured here last Tuesday, like, absolute buckets. The gutters on my house were totally overflowing. I really need to get up on a ladder and clean those out. The last time I was on a ladder, I was helping my neighbor string up some patio lights. He bought these really nice Edison bulbs, the kind with the visible filaments. They look great at night, really gives his backyard this cool, vintage vibe. He likes to grill out there, makes these amazing ribs with a dry rub...\n\nWait, sorry. Mistakes in my work. Yeah, I don't really think I'm making careless mistakes, honestly. Just... mostly struggling to keep things organized.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like your inability to focus is affecting your overall well-being or daily life in a major way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's... it's actually getting pretty bad. I missed a major custom delivery recently, which I never do. And my shop records got so completely disorganized that I actually had to stop taking new work for now just to try and figure out what's going on. \n\nIt's weird how fast paper can pile up, you know? Like, I used to keep everything in these manila folders, color-coded by the type of metal. I bought this huge pack of folders from an office supply store that was going out of business. It was one of those big box places, they had signs up everywhere, like \"Everything Must Go.\" I ended up buying a ton of stuff I didn't need, like three staplers and a whole box of those little binder clips. Binder clips are actually super useful for holding pieces together when you're soldering, if you don't have the right clamps. Soldering is probably my favorite part of the job, honestly. You have to get the torch just right, get the metal to that perfect cherry-red color before the solder flows. If you heat it too much, the whole piece just melts into a puddle. I melted a silver ring once when I was first starting out, just completely ruined it. The guy I apprenticed under, he just laughed and told me to throw it in the scrap pile. He had this massive scrap pile in the corner of his shop, must have been worth thousands of dollars just sitting there...\n\nOh, right, my daily life. Yeah, so I had to stop taking new work. And my family has been checking on me a lot more lately, too. I guess they got worried about how far I’d slipped from my usual routines. I'm usually pretty on top of things, but right now it's just... it's just really hard to keep it all together.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're doing two or more things at the same time, like working on a task and also thinking about something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. That's actually... that's kind of what really brought me in. My mind just will not stay on one thing anymore. I'll be sitting at the bench, right? And I'll be looking at a customer email on my phone, but then I notice a tiny little saw mark on a bezel I was working on, so I pick that up. And then I hear a truck backing up outside, and I start wondering if it's the delivery guy with the new polishing wheels I ordered. \n\nI ordered those wheels from this supplier in New York. They have this massive catalog, like, it's literally the size of a phone book. Do they even make phone books anymore? I remember when I was a kid, my mom used to use the old yellow pages as a booster seat for me at the kitchen table. We had this big oak table, super heavy. My dad bought it at an estate sale from this old lady who used to breed show dogs. Poodles, I think. Standard poodles, not the little toy ones. The standard ones are actually really smart, they used to be hunting dogs in Germany or somewhere. I watched a documentary about that once. They would shave the back half of the dog so it wouldn't get waterlogged when it was swimming after ducks, but they left the fur around the joints to keep them warm. It's crazy how those haircuts started out as something practical and now it's just for show...\n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the bench. Yeah, so I jump from the email to the saw mark to the noise outside, and by the time I look back down, I realize I’ve completely lost the whole work order. I just can't keep my focus on what I'm actually supposed to be doing.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain your relationships with friends due to your current struggles with focus and organization?\n\nPatient: Relationships with friends? I don't know, I haven't really noticed it being an issue. I haven't seen them as much lately, but I think that's just because everyone is so busy. My buddy Dave, he just got this new job working at a brewery across town. He's learning how to operate these massive stainless steel fermentation tanks. He was telling me about the whole brewing process the other day, how they have to carefully control the temperature when they add the yeast, otherwise it completely changes the flavor profile of the beer. Yeast is actually fascinating, it's a living organism, you know? My grandmother used to bake her own bread every Sunday, and she had this sourdough starter that she kept alive for, like, twenty years. She kept it in this old ceramic crock on the kitchen counter. It smelled so sour, kind of like vinegar but sweeter. She made the best sandwiches with that bread, usually with leftover roast beef from dinner the night before. She always used this really sharp horseradish mustard that would clear your sinuses right out...\n\nBut anyway, yeah, I don't think my focus stuff is messing up my friendships. We just haven't hung out much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focusing and staying on task is something that you've experienced throughout your life, or is it a relatively new development?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely new. I mean, I've always been a little scatterbrained, but not like this. This whole wired, completely out-of-control stretch... it just started right after I won this award at a big jewelry show. Which was amazing, by the way. It was a regional show, they held it in this huge convention center downtown. The lighting in those places is always terrible for displaying jewelry, it's all fluorescent overheads that wash out the stones. You really need halogen or good LED spot lighting to make the facets pop, especially on diamonds or sapphires. I had this one piece, a custom sapphire pendant, and I actually brought my own little battery-powered display lights just to make sure it looked right. Those little lights take these tiny button batteries, the kind that cost like five dollars each at the pharmacy. I always wonder who invented those. They're so specific. My watch uses one of those too, it's an old mechanical watch my grandfather gave me. Well, it's a quartz movement, so not truly mechanical, but it has that vintage look. He bought it in Switzerland back in the seventies when he was traveling for work. He used to sell industrial farm equipment, like those massive combine harvesters. I saw one of those up close once at a state fair, the tires on it were taller than I am. They had this whole display of antique tractors too, painted bright green and red...\n\nOh, but yeah, the focus thing. It started right after that jewelry show award, and it went on for about two weeks straight where I was just completely wired like that. So no, it's not a lifelong thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to pay attention to details or make careless mistakes in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the details are just... they're slipping right past me. It's so frustrating because my whole job is details. Like, I’ll stare at a repair ticket or a tray of sapphires and I just can’t hold the measurements in my head long enough to decide what to do next. Sapphires are actually really tricky to match, you know? People think they're just blue, but there are so many different shades. You've got your cornflower blue, your royal blue, and then the ones from Montana that have this almost steely, greenish tint to them. I actually really like the Montana ones. My uncle lives out in Montana, not too far from Yellowstone. He's got a cabin out there. We went to visit him when I was in high school and we saw this huge herd of bison just walking right down the middle of the road, stopping all the traffic. They're massive animals, it's terrifying being that close to them in a little rental car. The rental car was this tiny silver sedan, the air conditioning barely worked. Which was miserable because it was July and it gets surprisingly hot out there during the day. You don't think of Montana being hot, but the sun just beats down on you... \n\nBut yeah, I look at the stones and the tickets and I just can't keep the numbers straight in my head to actually do the work.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be in constant motion?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. During that whole stretch, I just... I couldn't sit still at the bench at all. I'd be bouncing around, you know? Like, I'd start sketching out this new watch case idea, and then two minutes later I'm emailing suppliers, and then I'm out on the floor rearranging the display trays, and I'd be doing that long after we closed for the day. Displays are actually really important, you have to group things by metal type or it looks cluttered. White gold and platinum need to be separated from the yellow gold. Although rose gold is getting really popular again. It's got copper mixed in, that's what gives it that pink color. My sister bought this huge set of copper pots and pans for her kitchen. They look beautiful hanging up, but they're a nightmare to keep clean. You have to polish them constantly or they tarnish and get all dull. She uses this special paste, it smells awful, kind of like rotten eggs. She made me help her polish them right before Thanksgiving dinner last year. We had this massive turkey, and my mom made her famous stuffing with the sage and sausage...\n\nBut yeah, I was just constantly moving, bouncing from one thing to the next, long after I should have gone home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty with focusing is more because you get easily distracted, or because you just can't seem to sit still and keep your mind on one task?\n\nPatient: It's... well, it's really the distraction. That's actually what brought me in, because my mind just won't stay on one thing anymore. Like, I'll be sitting at the bench, right? And I'll be looking at a saw mark on a piece of silver, and then I'm thinking about a customer email I need to answer, and then I hear a truck outside... and suddenly I realize I've completely lost the whole work order. I don't even know where I put the paper. And speaking of trucks, they've been doing all this road construction right outside the shop. They have those massive dump trucks backing up all day with that loud beeping noise. Beep, beep, beep. It drives me crazy. They're tearing up the whole intersection to put in new water mains or something. The pipes they're putting in are huge, like three feet across, made of this thick black plastic. I remember when my parents had to get the plumbing redone in our old house because the roots from this giant oak tree in the front yard had grown right into the clay pipes. That tree was beautiful, though. In the fall, the leaves turned this incredible deep red color, almost like a garnet. Garnets are actually really underrated stones. People think they're just cheap birthstones for January, but you can get them in all sorts of colors, even green. Tsavorite garnets are this amazing bright green, almost like an emerald but with better brilliance. I had a client ask for a tsavorite engagement ring once... \n\nBut yeah, the distraction is the main thing. I just jump from one thought to the next and lose track of what I'm actually supposed to be doing at the bench.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing these focus and distraction issues all the time, or are there periods where you feel more in control and focused?\n\nPatient: It's definitely not all the time. It comes in phases, I guess. Like, that whole wired, totally out-of-control stretch where I couldn't focus on anything? That started right after I won this award at a big jewelry show, and it lasted for about two weeks straight. The show was down in Miami, which was wild. I'd never been to Miami before. The humidity there is just insane, it hits you like a wet blanket the second you walk out of the airport. But the art deco buildings down on South Beach are incredible. All those pastel colors—the mint greens and the pale pinks—they look like ice cream. I actually got mint chocolate chip ice cream while I was walking down Ocean Drive. It melted so fast I had to eat it in like two minutes, and it dripped all over my favorite shirt. It was this vintage silk button-down I found at a thrift store back home. Thrift stores are great for finding weird, unique stuff. I once found this old brass pocket watch from the 1920s in a bin for like five bucks. The mainspring was busted, but the engraving on the back was just beautiful, this really intricate floral pattern...\n\nBut yeah, that really intense, distracted period went on for about two weeks after the show before things kind of shifted again."
